He thinks ADHD is overdiagnosed in energetic boys who need to move. He personally avoids painkillers and won't get LASIK.
PHI-17 | The dose makes the poison | Stress, training and every good thing become harmful in excess, and some things are harmful even in small amounts. Never being stressed leaves you bored; too much breaks you.
PHI-18 | Daily health habits | Walk every day in any weather; say out loud on a healthy morning that it is a good day; eat with others and without screens. Break up sitting with short walks; the "sitting kills" study ignored activity.
PHI-19 | Breathe through the nose | Breathe in through the nose always, in life and training: it filters germs and warms and humidifies air. "Mewing" is just normal tongue posture with no data that it changes adult faces.
PHI-20 | Posture has no quick fix | Strengthen traps (face pulls), abs and lower back, use a lumbar pillow, and hold posture deliberately (shoulders back, chin tucked, screens at eye level).
PHI-21 | Sun, heat and grooming | Build sun tolerance progressively and get some daily; use a sun lamp in gloomy climates; no fake tans; he thinks many sunscreens disrupt hormones, so limit exposure. In heat, gulp water between sets, use a fan, acclimatise and salt for electrolytes. Prefer cold showers and natural soaps.
PHI-22 | Lifting protects health | Lifting lowers blood pressure and the risk of cancer, diabetes and heart attacks, builds muscle you carry into old age and adds resilience to accidents (neck muscles in a crash). Gaming is catabolic because hours of sitting shrink muscle (his expectation, not studied).
PHI-23 | Disease impedes the body, not the will | The real damage of illness comes when the mind syncs with the weakened body. Treating mental-health issues as unable to stop your will makes you a tougher opponent for them.
PHI-24 | Pleasure must follow effort | Pleasure should be the by-product of and reward for effort, never pursued for its own sake. Pleasure is a spike and a valley; happiness is constant. Pleasures not preceded by effort ("eating out of boredom") are toxic.
PHI-25 | Enjoy pleasures without depending on one | Enjoy pleasures on your own terms, but don't make any one of them your only source of happiness, so you can lose it without pain. Stoicism is not mortification.
PHI-26 | Struggle is necessary | Life works like progressive overload; removing struggle makes people miserable. Struggle breaks you or tempers you. Seek out the "tiger" modernity removed and get things done in the storm rather than waiting for it to pass.
PHI-27 | Comfort corrodes; improve by subtraction | Self-improvement should mostly mean removing comforts and habits, not adding routines. Society shields people from hardship for decades; he believes we are in the "weak men create hard times" phase. Training against the elements strengthens body and mind.
PHI-28 | Hard work always improves results | Hard work doesn't guarantee winning but always improves results; working hard without working smart is slower and riskier. "No time" is false: we have more free time than ever and spend it on screens.
PHI-29 | Less effort, less gratitude | The less you work for something, the less grateful you are for it: lottery winners become miserable, spoiling someone removes their reason to strive, participation trophies devalue reward.
PHI-30 | Don't resign yourself to mediocrity | Giving up has become cool and victim mentality is comfortable; resigning yourself to mediocrity in your 20s means giving up on life. People resent self-improvers because it makes them accountable.
PHI-31 | The drive is internal | Owning a squat rack doesn't guarantee legs and lacking one doesn't prevent them; don't be limited by preconceived uses of tools.
PHI-32 | Feed good desires, starve the rest | Desire is not bad; it sets you in motion. Feed desires that are good for you and your community and starve the rest. Trendy renunciation can mask passivity, because ignored desires fester.
PHI-33 | Contemplate your death daily | Contemplate death every day, as the samurai and Stoics did. Accepting it is "the first day of your real life", removes fear and makes limited time matter; it is not nihilism. Every fear is ultimately fear of death.
PHI-34 | Avoid death but don't fear it | Keep self-preservation, but fear of death ruins quality of life. "You might die tomorrow anyway" is no way to plan; control what you can. Mortality gives life its flavour, and wanting immortality is childish.
PHI-35 | Live for convictions you'd die for | A man unwilling to die for his convictions is not willing to live for them. Find the one thing you love enough to accept it killing you. He would die trying to save his family.
PHI-36 | Grieve without numbing | Grief comes in waves that shrink over time; don't numb it with alcohol, drugs, rebounds or partying. Grief is the price of love and always worth it, so spend time with your living parents.
PHI-37 | Fame is worthless; impact counts | Judge success by your positive impact on others, even if nobody credits you. You take nothing to the grave except that impact. Early fame derails people.
PHI-38 | Believe in free will completely | There is no destiny; bad genes and an external locus of control are excuses. Believing in total free will is "naive but brave", believing you have none is cowardly. Life is poker: play the hand you're dealt.
PHI-39 | Uprightness is a choice | Environment makes being upright easier or harder but never impossible; worth comes from what you pursue, not birth. Being smart and having gym drive depend more on education and environment than on birth.
PHI-40 | Responsibility makes you an adult | Accepting responsibility is what makes you an adult; avoiding it produces Peter Pan (puer aeternus) men. Becoming a man takes work and a journey out into the world.
PHI-41 | Events are neutral; control the present | Your opinion of events causes the suffering. Only the present can be controlled, so don't lament the past or worry about fate or one-in-a-million events. Rehearse realistic scenarios once, then let them go.
PHI-42 | Own your feelings toward others | Negative feelings toward people are within your control; blaming others for them escapes responsibility. Blaming the system for failures while claiming credit for successes is pathetic.
PHI-43 | Build your identity alone first | Build yourself through many uncomfortable hours of solitude, then return to the group as an asset. Don't stay a permanent hermit or "sigma". "Become your own best friend."
PHI-44 | Strong individuals make strong collectives | Individualism is not selfishness: you build yourself in order to be useful. His balance sits closer to the individualistic side; the isolated lone wolf becomes pathological, and healthy masculinity is community-driven.
PHI-45 | Group identity is a comfortable trap | A group identity is borrowed and comfortable, and that comfort is the trap. A healthy group lets you exist outside it and leave; leave any group that does nothing for you. Cults use love bombing and constant activity.
PHI-46 | Don't tie identity to anything you can lose | Don't tie your identity to a job, possessions, a belief, sobriety, a diagnosis or a trauma. Losing them would crush you, a trauma made into identity can't heal, and a belief that became identity is almost impossible to correct.
PHI-47 | Walk your own path | Never hand your free will to another man: your own worst decisions about your life beat someone else's best. Don't worship anyone, NH included. Decide what you want before self-improvement, or you'll copy influencers' goals.
PHI-48 | The self is built, never finished | The true self is built, not found, and development ends only at death. Later framed as Jungian individuation, with the self ruling the ego.
PHI-49 | Wear masks, don't become them | Wear a social mask when needed, especially at work or in academia, but never let it become you. Outsiders should learn what makes "normies" tick. True love only blossoms when both partners drop their masks.
PHI-50 | Ignore the crabs in the bucket | Anyone with a real goal meets people who pull them down because success removes their excuses; set sail anyway and surround yourself with go-getters. Hiding your accomplishments only keeps others comfortable in mediocrity.
PHI-51 | Change your environment to change yourself | Changing your environment is one of the few real ways to change yourself. Leaving home alone to study in the US "made him a man", and he thinks every young man needs something similar.
PHI-52 | Kindness only counts from the strong | Kindness is a virtue only in someone who could be a tyrant. Become strong, then choose to be kind; a weak person's niceness is camouflaged cowardice. "Kind without strong is weakness, strong without kind is useless."
PHI-53 | Masculinity is contained strength in service | Healthy masculinity is great strength contained and put at the service of others, especially the weak: a dog with a wolf's power, never a sheep. By 2025 he defines masculinity as essentially providing service.
PHI-54 | "Toxic masculinity" is imbalance | Toxic masculinity is too little or too much, not masculinity itself. Only masculinity can stop masculine violence, so don't amputate its dark side; refine natural aggression into protection. Masculinity is shield and sword.
PHI-55 | A man is made, not born | Masculinity is rooted in hormones but needs culture, oral tradition and rites of passage. France abolished military service and has no rite of passage left, so men stay boys.
PHI-56 | Masculinity can't be bought | Masculinity is internal and free. Fitness products, supermarket meat, guns and hyper-masculine personas only sell an imitation.
PHI-57 | Fatherless society; learn from family men | Young men without role models fall for hyper-masculine online caricatures and gurus with no lasting families. Take advice from older men with families; raising kids and building something is manlier than glorified dying young.
PHI-58 | Men need the whole emotional range | Being a man includes crying and grief. Emotional concealment is "the number one reason" men are not doing well, and repressed emotion explodes onto loved ones. Male loneliness is real.
PHI-59 | Reclaim anger that fuels the will | Modern men are over-domesticated and need their fire back. Embrace anger if it fuels your will without letting it consume you; Stoic anti-anger suited fiery ancient men, not soft modern ones. An angry look is a social warning that prevents violence.
PHI-60 | Self-declared alphas aren't alphas | Alphas take position by force but keep it through care and example; alpha/sigma typologies are "astrology for men". Hierarchies are social and mobile.
PHI-61 | Real pride is internal | Healthy pride is closer to narcissism than society admits, but boasting unprompted shows insecurity; true experts don't announce it. Pride becomes arrogance when you stop learning or crush others.
PHI-62 | Independence and self-sufficiency | Independence is a core male quality: refuse handouts, which he calls traps, because men are treated as expendable. A grown man cooks, cleans and does his own laundry; a 28-year-old who can't cook is "pathetic".
PHI-63 | Every man should be able to defend himself | Violence is neutral and part of life; believing you'll never face it is childish. If good men are pacified, no one stops the bad ("si vis pacem para bellum"). Martial arts can be a door into physical culture.
PHI-64 | Self-defence rules | Fight back rather than submit; never follow an attacker to a second location; in a mugging, don't show fear or pretend nothing is happening. If followed, stay in light and among people. Aggressors look for prey that won't resist.
PHI-65 | Suppressed violence turns psychological | Suppressed physical violence becomes psychological violence and passive aggression, which he calls worse than open confrontation. Bullies only stop when met with matching force, and zero-tolerance policies make bullying worse; don't become a bully after being bullied.
PHI-66 | Strict boundaries get respected | Most people are neutral; your weakness turns them into jerks. Predators sense weakness, and boundary-pushers are cowards who stop when called out. Women must be twice as strict because they lack physical strength to back boundaries up.
PHI-67 | Never hit a woman except to protect life | Never hit a woman except to protect a life; if constant nagging becomes psychological violence, leave.
PHI-68 | "Man vs bear" statistics he cited | He cited figures he says show men are far more violent than women but also most homicide victims themselves, and recommends women read *The Gift of Fear*.
PHI-69 | Lifting won't make you a fighter | Fighting is mostly skill. To fight better, train fighting; lifting only complements it. Yet the idea that weights make fighters slow is a myth holding back karate and judo coaches.
PHI-70 | Max size and max skill don't mix | You can't be maximally big and maximally athletic or skilled. BJJ in particular is so time-consuming that non-novices can't keep growing muscle.
PHI-71 | Strike power comes from the hips | Strikes are powered by hip rotation with feet planted, not the arm; being big doesn't guarantee winning. A 500 lb box squat has little to do with kick power, flying kicks are rare in real fights, and a punching tower that blocks hip rotation has no carryover.
PHI-72 | Street arts and combining with lifting | For the street: baseline striking plus some wrestling (boxing, savate, muay thai); avoid ground arts there. When starting kickboxing, combine it with lifting, ease in, then shift energy toward it. Wrestling is itself resistance training.
PHI-73 | Athletes: sport first, then gym | Priority: play the sport, then drills, then hypertrophy. Athletes need minimal programs that carry over; weight training builds injury resilience and strength but is never as specific as practice. Keep some gym work in season and test speed and agility regularly. Mention outside sports when submitting a program.
PHI-74 | Question morality; judge by consequences | Morality is man-made, so question it constantly and judge morals, people and theories by consequences, not intentions. A toxic morality must be fixed, not abolished; judge one by whether you can live with its consequences (amor fati).
PHI-75 | Master versus slave morality | The weak invert values so the strong become "evil", and victimhood becomes the moral weapon of those who can't win on argument. Nietzsche's answer is to get strong yourself. Swole-shaming follows the same logic.
PHI-76 | Whatever expands you is good | Will to power is the never-satisfied drive to expand, not "big muscles or being an alpha". Growing embraces life; shrinking from pain leads toward depression. The Übermensch creates his own values but doesn't reject morality, and is an unreachable ideal; unlike Nietzsche, NH thinks everyone can break their conditioning.
PHI-77 | Nietzsche is misread | Nietzsche was not a relativist, anti-religion or a nihilist; he questioned morals rather than abolishing them, and "God is dead" was a diagnosis, not a celebration.
PHI-78 | Virtue is costly action, not signalling | Virtue is internal and shows in costly action; those who advertise their goodness usually lack it. Tolerance is passive and costs nothing, and being nice is not a virtue. Naturals who stay natural are simply living the virtue.
PHI-79 | Lead by example, not preaching | Virtue is taught by example; proselytising never works. A good father lets his kids see him train and treats their mother tenderly.
PHI-80 | Do good without seeking reward | Chasing kickbacks corrupts you and helping for interest is impure. He treats money lent to friends as gone and gives rather than lends.
PHI-81 | Hold a few firm principles | Hold a few firm principles even against the majority; moral relativism produces spineless people who can be sold anything, and a principle dropped when inconvenient was never yours. Nuancing a belief over time is adapting, not flip-flopping. He refuses ads and sponsorships on principle.
PHI-82 | Vice is a parasite | Vice is a parasite, not who you are; reject it in yourself rather than flee it in others. Lust and greed top his ranking of sins because most people suffer from one or the other. Vice and virtue are contagious.
PHI-83 | High standards and earned hierarchy | He defends elitism: high standards create excellent people and inequality drove humans to strive. Healthy hierarchies are earned, unfair ones collapse; equality is a man-made construct, dangerous when mistaken for nature. Shun obesity, smoking and drugs but help people overcome them with compassion.
PHI-84 | Be sincere and keep your promises | Be sincere and frank; he refuses to lie because tracking lies is too tiring. Keep your promises, to yourself first; the test is whether you can look at yourself in the mirror. Small protective lies exist in every relationship.
PHI-85 | No forgiveness without an apology | Everyone deserves a second chance on two conditions: they ask for it and they make an effort.
PHI-86 | Stoicism is controlled emotion, not coldness | Properly understood, Stoicism means feeling emotion and controlling it, living as an exemplary citizen, and applying it all day. Acceptance applies only to the truly unavoidable; many self-described stoics are smug misreaders.
PHI-87 | Stoicism is a starter, not the answer | Stoicism is a self-imposed tyranny that passionate ancient men built to tame their chaos; its contempt for the body is wrong and online Stoicism is dogmatic. It remains his recommended entry point. He admits he couldn't apply Stoic indifference to losing his family.
PHI-88 | Philosophy must be applied | Unapplied philosophy is "mental masturbation", like collecting gym equipment and never training, and it can poison you by making you feel smart. Never call yourself a philosopher or a stoic; a few short precepts beat volumes of theory.
PHI-89 | Read widely, not one book | Good philosophies complete each other; read those that make you angry. If one book represents you completely, you are basic. Specialisation pays in fitness but not in intellect.
PHI-90 | His reading method | Read the book yourself before hearing his take. Read once for style, reread chapter by chapter with a pen, then write a summary; if you can teach it, you understand it. With little time, read first thing in the morning and before bed.
PHI-91 | Reading order he recommends | Start with the Stoics (*Meditations* → *Enchiridion* → Seneca's *On the Happy Life* → Epictetus' *Discourses*), not Mishima; then Nietzsche (start with *Beyond Good and Evil*), Jung and Dostoevsky. Early picks: *Book of Five Rings*, *Hagakure*. Others: Adler, *The Gift of Fear*, Campbell, Debord, *48 Laws of Power* "if only to resist manipulation".
PHI-92 | Teach by giving keys | Teach by giving keys, not answers. If you can't explain something clearly you don't understand it, and hoarded knowledge is worthless. A good teacher is a perpetual student, patient, and aims to be surpassed.
PHI-93 | Musashi and Bushido ideas he keeps | Be able to state your Way of life without hesitating; know one thing deeply and you know ten thousand; rhythm matters more than speed; practise peripheral awareness; think 15 years ahead (Hagakure).
PHI-94 | No absolute truth, keep seeking it | Nobody is objective ("objectivity is subjectivity enough people agree on"); accept your subjectivity but keep pursuing objectivity. Whoever controls the media mass-produces "truth".
PHI-95 | Seek out opposing ideas | Question what society agrees on and seek opposing ideas with egos out of the debate; disagreement creates knowledge, echo chambers create fanboys. A theory is worth something only if it can be refuted. Don't blindly worship scientific authority.
PHI-96 | Stupidity is refusing to change your mind | Stupidity is refusing to change your mind, not ignorance. Be intelligent rather than appear so; emotional intelligence can matter more than IQ.
PHI-97 | Ignore the intellectually dishonest | Don't engage the intellectually dishonest; an insult needs your permission to hurt. Online meanness reveals the writer's insecurity. Criticise ideas, not names.
PHI-98 | Study influence to resist it | Study how influence works so you can't be manipulated; being pigeonholed in one ideology makes you easy prey. People only change when they think the idea is theirs. Manipulation is "the strength of the weak".
PHI-99 | Cut your news intake | Stop consuming so much news and chatter, especially political content on social media, which persuades you that you have enemies. Constant information makes people miserable.
PHI-100 | His own religious stance | He moved from baptised, non-practising Catholic to "agnostic but spiritual". He was influenced by Catholicism, Sunni Islam and nature; he studied Islam at 15-16 but couldn't accept giving up free will. When he prays he gives thanks rather than asks.
PHI-101 | Judge religion by what it builds | Judge a religion by how it structures society and lives, not literal truth. Believing in something beats believing in nothing. Science explains the material world, religion the spiritual, and humans need both; viewers with faith should join a congregation.
PHI-102 | Faith needs sacrifice and gratitude | Faith without pain or sacrifice is empty and watered-down religion is a shell. Real faith rests on gratitude, not fear.
PHI-103 | The West lost faith, kept new gods | Religion was mankind's "refinery" that tamed barbarians into chivalrous men. The modern West kept Christian morality but dropped the faith, so money, science, celebrities and hedonism became new gods; most modern neuroses come from a lack of spirituality.
PHI-104 | Read scripture as allegory | Read religious texts as allegory judged by the norms of their time; following any text to the letter is impossible and unwise. Be wary of religions promising a better afterlife, since it stops people improving on earth.
PHI-105 | Integrate your shadow | Every man has demons; acknowledge the shadow, integrate it and keep a grip on it, because denying it makes it get loose. Lifting is one way to integrate it. Left too long, it petrifies, and no book can do shadow work for you.
PHI-106 | The psyche self-regulates | Conscious and unconscious must stay balanced, like blood sugar; dreams and neuroses compensate for a one-sided conscious attitude. Productivity culture overvalues the conscious mind.
PHI-107 | Keep a dream journal | Keep paper by the bed and write dreams on waking, because the conscious mind erases them within minutes. An interpretation is valid if it resonates with the dreamer and gets them unstuck; a therapist who tells you what your dream means is a bad one.
PHI-108 | Become your own analyst | Choose a therapist who gives action items, avoids rushing to pills and doesn't feed transference; talk without action is spiralling. Speak about what festers (suicidal thoughts, jealousy, resentment), since speaking takes its power away; euphemisms push it into the unconscious.
PHI-109 | Loss of meaning is today's neurosis | Loss of meaning is the general neurosis of our time; spiritual death usually precedes suicide.
PHI-110 | Happiness is a choice made now | Deferring happiness until a goal is reached leaves you just as miserable; shrink the list of things you need. Progress toward happiness is slow and noticed in hindsight, like lifting.
PHI-111 | Gratitude is a daily habit | Gratitude separates the wise from fools; it is an active daily habit, not a feeling. Be grateful every day if you were born in the first world.
PHI-112 | Find a purpose; the ultimate one is love | Everyone would be happier with a purpose; if you have none, your purpose is to find one. By 2024 he says the ultimate purpose of life is love. If you can't say what you're doing with your life, you're going through the motions.
PHI-113 | Nihilism is self-destructive | Nihilism and the black pill are self-destructive: digest the bleak truth and turn it into a weapon. His ranking: active pessimist best, then active optimist, passive optimist, and passive pessimist worst.
PHI-114 | Suffering is not evil | "A true man says life is suffering with a smile." Amor fati means accepting hardship without becoming cruel.
PHI-115 | Escape through books, not screens | Escapism into artificial paradises verges on nihilism; if you must escape, books are healthiest because they take effort and have no algorithm. Transhumanism is escapism: optimise the biology you have first.
PHI-116 | Decision and time tools | Coin-flip trick (the side you hope for is your decision); spend little time on big one-off decisions and more on small recurring ones; cut sunk costs early; never apply a permanent solution to a temporary problem. Write your schedule down and cut what's unnecessary; waking early doesn't make you productive.
PHI-117 | Own tools, not possessions | Possessions end up possessing you. People in their 20s should buy as little as possible; consumption is about forgetting yourself. Sports cars and watches are the "worst self-improvement advice".
PHI-118 | Money is a tool | Money is a tool and necessary evil; earn enough for bills, savings and family, since chasing more fills a spiritual void. Capitalism is fine, but selling principles for wealth means money owns you.
PHI-119 | A job is for making a living | Take a tolerable job that leaves time for hobbies and family; don't monetise your passion or chase a dream job. If your job feels pointless, find meaning outside it. Drifting into a passionless job that swallows your identity is alienation.
PHI-120 | Career tactics | Pursue degrees only with clear job prospects; study what you love because you'll compete with people who do; no loyalty to companies (get raises with competing offers); apply everywhere and ignore listed requirements; admit you can't do a task rather than fail it.
PHI-121 | His stance on monetising his work | He refuses ads and sponsorships and has "a moral problem" charging for help; from 2025 he offers paid lessons that reward consistency. Supporters living paycheck to paycheck shouldn't donate.
PHI-122 | Love is always worth it | Love is the ultimate purpose and always worth it, even at the cost of betrayal. It is irrational, a responsibility you impose on yourself; love is equal exchange while adoration is one-sided.
PHI-123 | Lust is a firework, love a fire | Lust is the necessary first step and love is what remains when novelty fades; losing feelings right after sex means it was lust. What men call lust is often a need for touch.
PHI-124 | Build yourself before a relationship | A girlfriend won't cure loneliness: "to find the one, be the one". A couple must stay two individuals or becomes codependent. You are the most important person in your life until you have kids.
PHI-125 | Marriage is for life | Marriage is sacred and for life; he is a "fierce defender" of it. A soulmate is made through commitment, not found. Young men's fear of love is really fear of vulnerability.
PHI-126 | Relationship timeline | Learn to talk to girls as a teen, date lightly young, don't rush marriage, and never marry someone you haven't lived with. Being single in your late 20s or 30s is not late.
PHI-127 | Sex belongs in commitment | Delay sex to build connection; serial casual sex and hookup culture damage both sexes. Sex 50 times with one partner builds something, with 50 people nothing.
PHI-128 | Meeting women: initiate and learn by doing | You learn by approaching and taking rejection, not from YouTube theory. Build a connection first, practise with low stakes and escalate before the friend-zone window closes. Meet women through friends, shared hobbies, classes and female-dominated activities; avoid dating apps and workplace dating.
PHI-129 | Dates | The best first date is a walk in a nice, populated area: low-stakes, free, easy to leave. He never pays on a first date; if a date is cancelled the next step is hers; end with a gesture that sets up the next date. Consent: move slowly, accept "no" and continue only on clear signals.
PHI-130 | What attracts women, in his view | Confidence, competence, strength, humour and charisma (eye contact, posture, hygiene) attract; neediness, desperation and weakness repel. Looks matter a lot, with personality on top. He rejects pickup "game" and negging.
PHI-131 | Keeping a relationship alive | Keep dating your wife and don't turn submissive out of fear of losing her; hold firm under tests without anger or ultimatums; listen rather than solve; carry no resentment; it's the two of you against the problem. Political differences don't matter if fundamentals match.
PHI-132 | When to leave | Cheating, including emotional cheating, is a hard boundary. Leave a partner who insults or screams at you or threatens suicide to keep you; "a break" means it's over; never have kids with a drug user; fear of being alone is no reason to stay.
PHI-133 | Views on the sexes (his opinions) | Understand women through both biology and culture; women's emotional intelligence is a strength; rights come with duties; a trad wife is "built, not born" and men must embody those values first; men should be held to higher standards. He also made strongly gendered, contested predictions (his view only).
PHI-134 | Porn, not masturbation, is the problem | Porn is more addictive than steroids or stimulants, escalates, and destroys respect for women and the capacity for relationships; quitting it is "one of the most important self-improvement issues today". NoPorn is realistic where NoFap isn't. Quit with a pact, parental controls held by someone you trust, and thinking of the consequences.
PHI-135 | Controlled masturbation; NoFap is a meme | Masturbation is acceptable if controlled, imagination only and never sexual media. NoFap/semen retention began as a 4chan meme, is potentially dangerous, breeds resentment of women, and is useful only to break a porn addiction. Jelqing is pointless.
PHI-136 | Straight edge; no controlled use | He takes no drugs, alcohol or painkillers (refused them with broken ribs), except an occasional glass of red wine. "There is no controlled use": if you need it, the drug is in charge. Alcohol is the most dangerous drug because it is normalised. On policy, give real reasons rather than empty stigma or a coercive war on drugs.
PHI-137 | Video games: the unrecognised addiction | Video games are the number one addiction of young men with addictive personalities, copying real achievement's rewards without consequences. The more time a game demands, the worse; prefer finite, pausable games. MOBAs are the worst.
PHI-138 | Social media is poison for the young | Social media is "to women what porn is to men", and outrage-bait warps young men's view of women. Count hours on Instagram or porn as pure loss; TV with family can be legitimate recovery.
PHI-139 | Few friends, for life | Be extremely selective with friends, but friendship is for life. True friends tell hard truths and show up; friendship rests on shared virtue, not interest.
PHI-140 | Curate your circle | Seek asymmetric friendships (people above who help you, people below whom you help). Cut off narcissists, even family, and whoever holds you back. You can't save people who don't want saving; bad company is worse than solitude.
PHI-141 | Loneliness is your responsibility | Start conversations at cafés, libraries and the gym, create your own third spaces now that churches are gone, and don't isolate when you feel bad. Strong affectionate bonds between men are normal.
PHI-142 | Order your concern in circles | Yourself first, then family, community, country and the world last. People who claim to care about everyone are usually virtue signalling. Help when you can but preserve yourself.
PHI-143 | Children need love, challenge and boundaries | Children need structure, routine and firm boundaries; modern parenting makes the child royalty. Fathers should let boys take risks and roughhouse. The greatest gift is teaching the power of hard work. Raise children in a moral framework and let them choose a religion later.
PHI-144 | Your 20s set your trajectory | Use youth's spare time to discover who you are, enjoy your 20s without damaging your future, and master your emotions early. Finding what you want from lifting in your 20s is not too late; living with parents to save money while contributing is "not unmanly".
PHI-145 | No submission owed to abusive parents | You owe no submission to a bad or abusive parent; children should bide their time and leave rather than turn to violence. Honouring ancestors also means honouring the life you were given; blaming your genetics insults those who struggled so you could exist.
PHI-146 | Incels are made, not born | Inceldom is arrested development from mistreatment and missing rites of passage, so it can be reversed. Stop forum and black-pill channels, get a dog, and reintegrate. Bashing or banning incels reinforces the mentality; their real drive is acceptance and status, and the root is resentment where there should be gratitude.
PHI-147 | Modern humans are devolving | Humans are devolving physically, intellectually and morally by trying to exit nature and abolish suffering. Culture follows its myths: when its heroes turn mediocre, so do its people. Nostalgia is futile.
PHI-148 | Politically independent | He refuses any party's package, holding views from several camps, and puts freedom above security. He critiques democracy as rewarding the average. Lifters lean right through in-group values, and the left should drop its hostility to physical culture.
PHI-149 | Social and political claims he made | His own unverified opinions: against COVID vaccine mandates; the toilet-paper shortage was manufactured; Islam may become France's largest religion within ~20 years; a "fit dictatorship" would be dangerous; insect protein is economical but not sustainable, and food waste should be tackled first.
PHI-150 | His claims about real people | All his unverified opinions: he calls Zyzz an insecure drug addict and "the opposite of a role model"; says Liver King's tenets weren't dumb but the marketing was; says island-retreat gurus follow a cult pattern; argued a critic (Blaha) is closeted, conceding it is only a theory; and read Tate's chess anecdote as possible sadism.
CUL-01 | Anime and manga started his lifting | Anime and manga (Baki, Berserk, Hunter x Hunter, Yu Yu Hakusho, shonen training arcs) are the source of his lifting motivation and the reason the channel exists; his big-arm obsession comes from anime heroes.
CUL-02 | His first training came from manga | His first training was manga-style push-ups and sit-ups, starting from zero push-ups; shonen training arcs remain his favourite part of manga. Watching Dragon Ball or Baki growing up drives many of the biggest naturals.
CUL-03 | Fictional role models are valid | Motivation comes from realism (real athletes) or fiction (manga, myth); he draws mainly from fiction, and without real role models fictional ones are valid. Model yourself on a male character you admire rather than an anime "waifu".
CUL-04 | Fictional physiques are fine fuel | Fictional physiques are valid goals and motivation if you sort out what is natty-achievable; constant exposure to great physiques reminds you to strive. Fans copy heroes' physiques rather than their feats because the feats are impossible.
CUL-05 | His goal physique over time | His goal moved from adult Gon to a Zodd/Guts/Uvogin/Toguro mix (Guts has too much shoulder for his taste) to an explicitly unreachable anime body, which is why he keeps striving. Real-world favourites: Serge Nubret's physique and Sergio Oliva's arms.
CUL-06 | Realistic vs unrealistic fictional targets | Golden Age Guts and Toji (Jujutsu Kaisen) are realistic; other Guts arcs are too unrealistic or imbalanced; Yujiro is unachievable even with drugs. Many Baki physiques would need full bodybuilding specialisation, and being that big while staying nimble is impossible.
CUL-07 | Shonen is about self-improvement | The real goal of shonen is self-improvement; strength and physique are means. Physique and feats together inspire because form and function complete each other.
CUL-08 | Copying anime training doesn't work | Anime regimens are deliberately exaggerated; swinging a sword like Guts won't build his body, resistance training will. Fiction tropes are not methods: weighted clothing, cliff-jumping for reaction time, or the Solo Leveling daily quest (Saitama's joke routine followed unironically).
CUL-09 | His character programs | He offers a free Guts-physique program for those who only care about the look and planned a Casca program for women who want to be jacked. Halberd swings mimic a lat pulldown and standing Russian twists.
CUL-10 | Power fantasies are a healthy heritage | Male power fantasies show men who they wish to be. They have always existed (Greek and Norse myth, chansons de geste, Mahabharata, manga), give men a blueprint and keep masculine standards alive; shonen is today's most effective vehicle. Bodybuilding's admiration of the male body is a power fantasy, not lust.
CUL-11 | Earned power vs handed-out power | Healthy fantasies show earned growth (Luffy's training, Goku and Vegeta, Ippo). Bloodline and destiny weaken the message. Solo Leveling and isekai are toxic: random power means the hero isn't responsible for his growth, and it "works like porn". Values stick when shown through a hero's journey.
CUL-12 | Modern shonen heroes went soft | Modern shonen heroes are "crybabies" (male weakness fantasies) compared with hypermasculine 80s-90s heroes. Dragon Ball Super got worse because power-ups stopped showing as bigger bodies. In male power fantasies violence is never normalised; it fights violence or prepares for it.
CUL-13 | In Baki, training beats talent | Baki overtook the gifted Hanayama and Rihito's untrained grip stagnated; Baki trains in a large share of panels, which he reads as storytelling that training is the right way. Characters train regardless of bad days or weather.
CUL-14 | Jack Hanma is the PED cautionary tale | Jack is the only PED user in Baki and embodies the PED mindset and advanced bigorexia: living for the present and sacrificing his future. He never matches his natural father, so he's no role model; the rest are officially natural.
CUL-15 | Size vs skill in fighting fiction | Size and power remove the need for technique against average fighters and some muscle is necessary, but skilled, faster fighters beat giants, which is why weight classes exist. Baki's gains went to the upper back and posterior chain, which transfer ground force into strikes.
CUL-16 | Other lessons he reads into Baki | Baki shows men in their 50s still jacked; downsizing arcs show a leaner base with no strength loss; it covers body dysmorphia, depression and meaning and is grounded in realism. Its physiques escalate each arc like pro bodybuilding and lose charm in *Son of Ogre*.
CUL-17 | Guts is a tragic hero, not a template | Guts is a tragic hero, not an anti-hero. Young men copy his pathological traits (lone wolf, violence, the memefied stoic chad) instead of the virtues he tries to preserve. Young Guts used sword training and exhaustion to escape existential pain.
CUL-18 | Berserk's lesson is about relationships | Relationships anchor our humanity, so self-improvement should aim at becoming more loving. Berserk is a love story and, in his reading, a feminist work; its fans are emotionally sensitive, not gore addicts.
CUL-19 | Be an active pessimist and master your fate | He calls himself an active pessimist like Guts, prepared for failure, while active optimists like Griffith collapse when they fail. Learned helplessness (Corkus blaming talent) is the flaw to avoid. Revenge never heals.
CUL-20 | Yujiro is the shadow of masculinity | Yujiro is toxic hypermasculinity coded as what not to be; Baki is his opposite. The hyper-male (chaos, instinct) differs from the alpha (order, hierarchy). In his view the father brings challenge and the mother comfort.
CUL-21 | "Literally me" villains | Young men idolise villains like Patrick Bateman as catharsis for restricted masculinity. Bateman is surface-level (looks, muscle, money) and stands for men measuring worth by possessions.
CUL-22 | France's swole-shaming culture | France has a swole-shaming problem: "gonflette" exists only to mock lifters, Paris men lose muscle yearly, and he was once one of those "wimps". He calls France the "wimpiest country"; Hollywood's 80s jock-villain taught that big means bad. He links America's admiration of big men to a Protestant ethic.
CUL-23 | Bodies reflect minds in fiction | *300* is fair "masculine propaganda" that pushes men to act, the phalanx mirroring a high-trust society of strong individuals. In *Dune* the lean Atreides vs the obese Baron shows bodies reflecting minds. Mishima went from cerebral boy to man of physical culture through a deliberate plan.
CUL-24 | Physical-competition TV | A Korean physical-competition show models camaraderie and counters body positivity, but equating torso sculptures with capability is a trap. Strength-heavy tests skew against women; season 2 fixed the stamina emphasis.
CUL-25 | Vikings as the modern power archetype | Viking imagery dominates lifting culture as the modern archetype of power; Vikings needed exceptional strength for axe fighting and groomed well.
CUL-26 | Indian physical culture | Vedic archery and riding; epic-age javelin, club swinging and wrestling; a decline under Buddhist influence and British rule; revival in the 1920s.
CUL-27 | Medieval people trained hard | The average medieval man had a dedicated gym rat's development; exercise was social and outdoors. Knights trained from about age 10 as squires (armour carries, stone throws, weighted ladder pull-ups, presses).
CUL-28 | Sandow and the old strongmen | Sandow founded an Institute of Physical Culture with judged shows, sold mail-order plans (the first personal trainer) and was filmed by Edison. He recommends Desbonnet's book on old strongmen as proof of natural excellence. Steve Reeves credited breathing squats for ribcage expansion; worth testing.
CUL-29 | Gymnastics is "anti-powerlifting" | Gymnastics holds the hardest positions for aesthetic criteria; it began in Greece and was revived by German educators. Eastern European calisthenics strength reflects the parks available there.
CUL-30 | Let people work in | A gym is a community, not your property: let people work in on machines and stations; refusing on barbell lifts is understandable. When spotting, be patient and don't grab the bar early; you can decline to spot.
CUL-31 | Don't interrupt people mid-set | Don't talk to people mid-set or step into their sightline; headphones mean no chat. Don't give women unsolicited advice mid-set or watch them in the mirror. Never step over someone's barbell.
CUL-32 | Big regulars are supportive | In years of training he has never had a problem with a woman at the gym; offenders are almost always men. Women in male-dominated gyms are often defensive from discomfort. The biggest regulars are the most supportive, so novices needn't fear them. French gym-goers are ruder than Americans.
CUL-33 | Women's training is not a men's program in pink | Women should train more lower body than men and don't need the bench press (flyes can do the job). A real women's program was planned, "not a men's program painted pink". Female-only gyms rarely work because men pay most memberships.
CUL-34 | How he reviews programs | Read the lifter's goals and limitations first, then review the program "cold" because it must stand on its own. Paid physique reviews need many photos and videos. He only shares methods he has used and seen work. Write sets and reps with consistent notation.
CUL-35 | Measure with a tight tape | Measure with the tape pulled tight and no slack, at the thickest part (thigh) or flexed (arm); a loose tape is cheating.
CUL-36 | Read manga rather than watch anime | He prefers reading manga and usually watches only anime he has already read; Yu Yu Hakusho is the exception where the anime is better. Mocking anime fans reflects conformism; dismissing all anime is like dismissing all cinema.
CUL-37 | His favourite works | One Piece is the greatest manga of all time (Hunter x Hunter could have rivalled it). Eyeshield 21 is a top-3 sports manga with a science-based vs Spartan training arc; Shin is his favourite character, and like Guts, Zoro and Shin he prefers training alone. Dune is his favourite book (2024).
CUL-38 | Books he rereads | He has reread the *Book of Five Rings* every summer since 22 (it must be read between the lines) and ranks the Hagakure books 2 > 1 > 3.
CUL-39 | Miyazaki and film opinions | *The Boy and the Heron* is one of Miyazaki's worst films (rushed, nostalgia recycling) yet better than 99% of Japanese animation; he rewatches *Mononoke* and *Spirited Away* yearly.
CUL-40 | Grip and Eastern methods appeal to him | Grip strength is the expression of strength that draws him most, and a common manga power fantasy. He prefers Eastern training methods to Western ones.
CUL-41 | The channel is public domain | All channel content is public domain: no strikes, anyone may re-upload, and the best help is archiving. He would not recreate the channel if deleted. In 2022 Japanese companies struck his anime videos for months and YouTube auto-rejected his counter-claims; he considers his anime videos transformative fair use.
CUL-42 | Channel formats | Q&As are monthly (hypertrophy), injury every ~2 months and nutrition every ~3. Long videos are split. The philosophy series (Educated Barbarian) became rarer and longer, later moving to Jung streams and weekly Wednesday streams; background music was dropped by vote.
